Once you have looted the **Solid Skellige Sword** and examined the inscription, our objectives will update once again. Either use the nearby sail boat or return to shore and fast travel to the town of Svorlag on the northwestern island of Spikeroog.

In Svorlag, speak with the merchant by the docks in the southern part of town. During the conversation, hand over the sword to complete the side-quest.

> **Note:** For completing this side-quest you'll earn **25XP** and **55 Crowns**.

### Fists of Fury: Skellige

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Kaer Trolde Harbour** Notice board on Ard Skellig.

This is the third **‘Fist of Fury'** side-quest and like the previous ones in Velen and Novigrad it is short, relatively easy and probably won't cause you too much grief (as long as you are at the recommended level). The side-quest requires that you track down a series of challengers and best them in hand-to-hand combat.

As you approach each of the challengers, you need to speak with the Fightmaster or Bookie (depending on location) nearby to set up a fight. You can find the fighters in the following locations:

- **Valgard**–This fellow can be found outside of the inn Yennefer was staying in in Kaer Trolde Harbour. Before the fight, your opponent will ask you to take a dive – it's up to you whether you do or not (you can simply fight him again afterwards, so it's no big deal). When you get around to fighting him be warned – he will pretty much block any quick attacks you throw his way and can chain together flurries of quick attacks himself. The best strategy to beat him is to counter an attack and follow up with a strong attack before backing off and repeating the process.
- **Einar**– The second fighter is found in the town of Harviken on the island of Faroe, the southeastern of the Skellige isles. This fellow is fast on his feet and is extremely aggressive, constantly progressing on your position. He'll mix up heavy and quick attacks quite deftly into combos as well, so do your best to keep your distance. The best strategy here is to dodge when he attacks and immediately counter with a quick attack before backing off and repeating until he hits the deck.
- **Grim** – Grim can be found in the town of Arinbjorn on the far western side of the main island of Ard Skellig. Compared to the previous fights, Grim is an easier target. He is quite fast and can rattle off a few punches at you very quickly whilst also being able to counter you if you get greedy with your attacks. Simply dodge when he attacks and immediately counter with a quick attack or two before rinsing and repeating. This tactic will have him tits up in no time.
- **Olaf** – Olaf is the final challenge in this side-quest and is found just northeast of the town of Urialla Harbour on the island of An Skellig, the northeastern of the Skellige isles. He is large and definitely the trickiest of the fighters you'll face. In addition to bonus health, he has a large range on his melee attacks and can lunge at you from across the arena to attack. As his range is superior to Geralt's it's a good idea to do your best to keep your distance as much as possible. The best strategy here is to sidestep/roll to the side when he attacks and immediately chit him with an attack or two in the back before backing off before he can attack again. Keep that up and he'll be pushing up daisies in no time.

> **Note:** You'll earn **25XP** and **70 Crowns** per win for defeating each of the first three combatants and **50XP** and **90 Crowns** for defeating Olaf. As you progress through the fights, you'll also be rewarded with **Diagram: The Digger**, **Diagram: Nilfgaardian Guardsman Armor** and a **Steel Sword – Gwestog**.

> **Note:** For completing this side-quest you'll earn the **‘Brawler'** achievement/trophy.

> **Note:** Completing this side-quest will automatically kick off the next in the chain **‘Fists of Fury: Champion of Champions'**.

### Fists of Fury: Champion of Champions

**Start Location:** This side-quest will begin automatically after completion of **‘Fists of Fury: Skellige'**.

Once this quest has become active and you wish to partake in its questiness, you'll need to travel to the island of Spikeroog, the northwestern-most of the major islands in the Skellige Isles map. Our destination is the town of Hov which can be found on the northeastern tip of Spikeroog.

Upon arriving in Hov, look for the Bookie by the large totem in centre of town. Speak with him to trigger another side-quest ‘ **Master of the Arena'**. We'll need to complete this quest before we can continue.

After completing the **‘Master of the Arena'** side-quest, return to the Bookie to start the fight with the Champion of Champions.

This time around we'll be fighting a Rock Troll in hand to hand combat. As he is covered in rocky hide, he has a lot of additional armour so you'll need to land a lot more blows on him to dent his health. Similarly, to the bear you had to fight earlier, the Troll has a large range on his melee, can lunge at you from across the arena to attack, he'll also link quick attacks together to dish out some serious damage.

As his range is superior to Geralt's it's a good idea to do your best to keep your distance as much as possible. The best strategy here is to dodge backwards when he attacks and immediately smack him with a quick attack before dodging backwards again to quickly move out of range. Maintain this basic strategy throughout the fight and as long as you don't get lazy on your dodges or greedy on your attacks. you shouldn't have too many issues with taking him down.

> **Note:** For completing the quest you'll earn **50XP**, **90 Crowns** and **Diagram: The Tamer**.

> **Note:** For completing this quest, you'll earn the **‘Brawl Master'** achievement/trophy.

## Skellige Isles Witcher Contracts

There are a total of six Witcher Contracts to find and complete in the and its surrounds. Each of these contracts will be available for pickup from one of the game's notice boards placed in populated areas and townships around the region. The Witcher Contracts are listed below in order of quest level, hit the hyperlink to jump down to a walkthrough of the contract itself:

- **[Contract: Strange Beast](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20Strange%20Beast)** (Level 16)
- **[Contract: The Phantom of Eldberg](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20The%20Phantom%20of%20Eldberg)** (Level 17)
- **[Contract: Muire D'yaeblen](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20Muire%20D'yaeblen)**(Level 18)
- **[Contract: Here Comes the Groom](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20Here%20Comes%20the%20Groom)**(Level 19)
- **[Contract: Dragon](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20Dragon)**(Level 28)
- **[Contract: Missing Son](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Contract:%20Missing%20Son)**(Level 29)

### Contract: Strange Beast

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Larvik** Notice board on Hindarsfjall, the easternmost island of the Skellige Isles.

After grabbing the side-quest from the notice board in Larvik, you can start it by speaking with an NPC named Thorleif by the weapon/armour enhancement areas in town. You'll learn that his son is missing and it's up to us to find out what happened.

Following the conversation, make your way over to the northern side of town and speak with Sverre he and his buddy here about the contract. Once you have quizzed him using all the available conversation options, our objectives will update.

At this point, we'll want to make our way to the objective marker along the road to the west of Larvik and around halfway to the next town of Lofoten. After arriving at the site of the attack, examine the investigation area. Activate Geralt's Witcher Senses and explore the area. You can inspect the following points of interest:

- The **red clay** on the ground behind the cart.
- The **corpse** by the cart on the southern side of the road.
- The **pool of blood** on the northern side of the road opposite the cart.
- A **set of footprints** beside the pool of blood (will appear after examining the other items of interest).

Follow the footprints away from the attack site and a short distance into the woods. You'll come across a small cave. Drop down inside.

The initial tunnel is filled with poison gas, so make sure you have Geralt equipped with **AARD** or **IGNI**( **AARD** is preferable in the cramped space) and use the signs to clear the gas as you progress. You'll also encounter a Nekker or two as you go, so keep an eye out for them and stay away from the gas!

Eventually, the tunnel will empty into a small, open cavern at the end. Drop down to the floor and fight off the 4-5 Nekkers that will attack at this point. After defeating them, a short scene will play and a boss fight will commence.

> **Note:** The spelling of the boss's name is different between how it appears in the bestiary menu (Hagubman) and how it appears next to its health bar (Hagumban). I've elected to use the bestiary's spelling.

> ##### Boss: Hagubman (Nekker Warrior)
>
> The boss Nekker is quite similar to those you have fought in the game so far, he just has significantly more health and can do a lot more damage with his attacks. He performs the same general melee swipes and leap attacks as you would see from a regular Nekker, and by this stage you should be adept at blocking/dodging these!
>
> At several points during the fight Hagubman will burrow into the earth and summon a group of four level 13-14 Nekkers. We'll be required to defeat the group of Nekkers before the boss will return. The boss himself can be knocked down by a blast of **AARD**, stunned by **AXII**, slowed by **YRDEN** and will take damage from **IGNI**. His biggest weakness however is Northern Wind Bombs which will dish out significant damage.

Once the Nekker Warrior is dead, loot the body for the **Nekker Warrior Trophy**, **Nekker Warrior Mutagen** and any other goodies it was carrying.

With Hagubman now pushing up daisies, we can mosey on back to Larvik and speak with Thorleif once again to end the side-quest. During the conversation you can ask for more **Crowns** if you wish, or accept the reward as is.

> **Note:** for completing the side-quest, you'll earn **200XP** and **390 Crowns**.

### Contract: The Phantom of Eldberg

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Arinbjorn** Notice board in western, central Ard Skellig.

Once you have picked up the quest from the notice board, you'll notice an objective marker has appeared on the map on the small group of islands just to the north of Arinbjorn and to the south of Kaer Trolde Harbour. You can access them easily via any nearby boat, or alternatively taking the dirt path leading to the northwest from the town of Sund and swimming across to the islands at the closest possible point.

When you reach the island chain make your way north to the island with the lighthouse indicated by the objective marker, killing any wandering Pirates who may come across your path.

Upon arriving at the northern island with the lighthouse you'll notice a creepy fog set in. Follow the dirt path here towards the objective marker. As you proceed, you'll need to fight a pair of Wraiths that will appear and attack.

The objective marker will become a search area when you get closer. Stick on Witcher Sense and inspect the **dead body** in the middle of the road. Approach the nearby building and have Geralt knock on the door for a scene.

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

Following the conversation with Mikkjal exit the building and continue to follow the path until you reach the lighthouse. There will be another pair of Wraiths that appear and will attack as you go, so be prepared (and don't say I didn't warn you!).

The lighthouse itself represents one large search area which we can explore and investigate. Pop on Geralt's Witcher Senses and you and examine the following:

- **Symbols** scrawled on the wall of the lighthouse by the first set of stairs.

After inspecting the Symbols, Geralt will figure out what he is up against.

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

Backtrack to the previous building. Rest, meditate and prepare yourself for a boss fight before chatting with Mikkjal once again to trigger the fight.

> ##### Boss: Penitent (Wraith)
>
> The Penitent is quite similar to the Wraiths you have fought in the game so far, she just has significantly more health and can do a lot more damage with her attacks. The Penitent will perform the same general melee swipes and teleport attacks as a regular Wriath so you'll want to do your best to keep an eye out for attacks coming from odd locations.
>
> At several points during the fight the Penitent will split into a group of four level 13-14 Wraiths. We'll be required to defeat the group of Wraiths before the boss will return. The penitent herself can be slowed by **YRDEN** and can be tanked quite effectively with **QUEN**. Using **Moon Dust Bombs** can cause significant damage.
>
> For the first little bit of the fight, the boss is extremely hardy and will do increased damage and take next to none from your melee attacks. As such, do your best to keep your distance and avoid attacks like the plague! After a minute or so, Mikkjal will light the fire in the lighthouse and at this point the boss will weaken significantly, allowing you to take her out as you would a normal Wraith.

Once the Penitent is dead, loot the body for the **Wraith Trophy** and any other goodies it was carrying. A scene will play.

After one final chat with Mikikjal, return to Arinbjorn and speak with Jorund in the inn to end the side-quest.

> **Note:** for completing the side-quest, you'll earn **260XP** and **184 Crowns**.

If you complete this side-quest before completing **'King's Gambit'** as you attempt to leave the pair of Skelligers who weren't too pleased with your arrival will show up again and trigger a new side-quest **'Stranger in a Strange Land'**.

### Contract: Muire D'yaeblen

**Start Location:** From Bjorg near the **‘Kaer Trolde Harbour'** fast travel point. Will appear after completing the **‘Destination Skellige'** main quest.

After speaking with Bjorg in Kaer Trolde harbour to kick off the contract, have a peek at your map. You'll notice that there are two objective markers to investigate – one along the docks near the ‘ **Kaer Trolde Harbour'** fast travel point and another, also along the docks to the west. Let's head to the travel marker first.

Speak with the Boatwright's Apprentice here who'll add a new objective marker to your map once you are done. Before making your way over to the new objective marker, speak to the other villager with information on the attack. Just to the west you'll find a woman who we can speak to working on some animal skins by a building. Her testimony will add another objective marker to the map.

At this point we'll now need to head out to examine both of the attack sites marked by objective markers nearby. Fortunately, both of these are located at waterfront/beach type locations, so to make things much quicker than a lengthy slog overland, we can pinch a boat from the harbour and sail down the coast to the south to visit both of them.

The attack site area to the Kaer Trolde Harbour is guarded by a pair of level 18 Drowned Dead (think bigger, more aggressive Drowners). Kill the pair of them before looting the chest in the middle of the area.

Use your Witcher Sense to locate the corpse on the northern shore of the yellow search area. Examining the **dead** **body** will have a scent trail appear when Witcher sense is active. Ignore the trail for now (unless you feel like swimming a long way through a dark underwater tunnel) and instead, lets head for the next objective marker for an easier way to find the Drowner's lair.

Hop back in the boat and sail it further to the south until you reach the second search area. At this location, bang on your Witcher Senses once again and locate the **crab pots** on the beach. Examining these will have a scent trail appear when Witcher Sense is active. This time around, follow the scent trail to the south until you find the entrance to a cave – this is the Drowners' lair!

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

Continue through the tunnel into the cave, looting the bodies that you come across as you go and using your Witcher Senses to examine the occasional body part. With Witcher Senses active, you'll be able to find a set of footprints leading into the first entrance we reach on the right.

Follow the footprints to the right and you'll encounter a group of 5-6 level 18-19 Drowned Dead. Make use of the swamp gas in the cave here with IGNI to damage as many enemies as possible before using your regular combat skills to eliminate the remaining monsters. In this room (if you are interested) you can examine a rusty piece of armour in the corner behind the swamp gas.

To continue, proceed down the small tunnel on the right side of the room. Examine the corpse here and proceed into the next larger cavern area. Here we'll be able to explore a little. Activate Witcher Sense and you can inspect the following:

- The assortment of **animal corpses** scattered around the room.
- The **human corpses** littered around the area.
- The section of **wrecked boat** on the left hand wall.

After examining all three items of interest, Geralt will guess what the monster behind the attack is and your objectives will update. Loot the chest in the section of the broken ship nestled on the right hand wall. Return to the search area. At this point we'll need to brew a new potion – **Drowner Pheromones**. Enter the Alchemy tab of the menu and brew yourself up a batch (if you haven't done so before).

Meditate and prepare yourself for a fight and when you are ready to continue, drink the **Drowner Pheromones** potion before hiding in the tunnels just ahead. This will trigger a scene after which there is a boss fight to partake in. Loot the pair of chests located in the short tunnels here before returning to the previous room to start the fight.

> ##### Boss: Abaya (Water Hag)
>
> Abaya is essentially a faster moving, more powerful version of your standard Water Hag. As such, she'll pretty much be limited to the normal Water Hag attack routine of sinking into the ground and appearing elsewhere if you get too close to her for too long. Her attacks include a standard melee swipe which she can chain together into brief combos and she'll also regularly throw projectiles at you and if they hit, they'll obscure the screen for a few moments.
>
> In addition to the main boss, she'll often be accompanied by a pair of level 18 Drowning Dead. Try to eliminate them first before focusing on the main threat.
>
> Defeating the Water Hag is relatively straight forward. You'll want to dodge her projectile attacks as you close ground to her before blasting her with **IGNI** or tanking her with **QUEN**. Dodge her retaliatory melee attacks and counter until she sinks into the water and appears elsewhere. Repeat the process until she's swimming with the fishes (both figuratively and literally!).

Once the Water Hag is dead, loot the body for the **Water Hag Trophy**, **Water Hag Mutagen** and any other goodies it was carrying.

With the boss now pushing up daisies, we can mosey on back Kaer Trolde Harbour and speak with Bjorg once again to end the side-quest. During the conversation you can ask for more **Crowns** if you wish, or accept the reward as is.

> **Note:** for completing the side-quest, you'll earn **200XP** and **187 Crowns**.

### Contract: Here Comes the Groom

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Svarlag** Notice board in on Spikeroog, the northwestern island of the Skellige Isles.

After grabbing the side-quest from the notice board in Svorlag, you can start it by heading out to the objective marker just outside of town and speaking with an NPC named Kevan. You'll learn that his broseph is missing and it's up to us to find out what happened.

Following the conversation, we'll have a new objective marker on the map. Hop on your horse and ride to the next point of interest north of town. Here we'll have another brief chat with an NPC named Britt who'll give you a new objective marker to investigate.

With all the chatting out of the way, it's time to get down to the monster hunting. We'll need to follow the path leading to the south from the town of Svorlag and past the ‘Old Watchtower' fast travel marker until we reach the beach in the southernmost part of Spikeroog island. Here we'll find a cave.

Enter the cave and once inside, switch on Geralt's Witcher Senses to locate a trail on the floor. Examine this and follow it a short distance into the cave. As you work your way along the initial tunnel and reach the first open room you'll encounter numerous Drowners (I counted six, but there may have been more!) so clear them as you go.

After arriving in the first cavernous area after the initial tunnel, examine the investigation area. Activate Geralt's Witcher Senses and explore the area. You can inspect the following points of interest:

- The **large red altar** in the centre of the area.
- The **scales** on the ground behind the altar.
- The **monster tracks** on the floor a short distance down the tunnel behind the altar.

Follow the monster tracks a short distance further into the cave and you'll come across another group of four Drowners. Dispatch them before climbing up the successive ledges to the passage leading back outside.

Upon exiting the cave, prepare yourself for a fight. When you are ready to continue, use Witcher Sense to find the body nearby and inspect it to trigger the boss fight.

> ##### Boss: Melusine (Ekhidna)
>
> Melusine is a bigger, larger form of the Ekhidnas we've fought before. She'll spend most of her time in the air swooping at you and performing melee attacks that will poison Geralt if they make contact.
>
> As with most flying monsters, the Ekhidna can be knocked from the sky with a blast of **AARD**. She's also quite weak to both the **IGNI** sign and **Grapeshot Bombs**, so feel free to throw those in her general direction once she has been knocked to the ground. You can also use your crossbow for some middling amounts of damage when she flies out of range of your other attacks.
>
> The trick to defeating the boss is to avoid her attacks (that poison is nasty!) and knock her to the ground with **AARD** before exploiting you silver swords and her other weaknesses to dispatch her as quickly as possible. Repeat as necessary.

Once the Melisune is dead, loot the body for the **Ekhidna Trophy**, **Ekhidna Mutagen** and any other goodies it was carrying. With the boss now pushing up daisies, we can return to Kevan near Svorlag once again and speak with him to end the side-quest.

> **Note:** for completing the side-quest, you'll earn **200XP** and **186 Crowns**.

### Contract: Dragon

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Fyresdal** Notice board in southern Ard Skellig.

After grabbing the side-quest from the notice board in Fyresdal, you can start it by speaking with an NPC named Vagn in town. You'll learn that there has been a recent Dragon attack and he'll request that we track it down and kill it.

Following the conversation, make your way over to the house nearby indicated by the objective marker. Speak with Karli inside about the contract. Once you have quizzed her using all the available conversation options, our objectives will update.

Switch on Witcher Senses and inspect the pair of **bodies** on the floor here.

Exit the building and look at the map. At this point, we'll want to make our way to the objective marker along the road to the north of Fyresdal. After arriving at the site of the attack, examine the investigation area. Activate Geralt's Witcher Senses and explore the area. You can inspect the following points of interest:

- **Dead cow** on the side of the road.
- The **blood splatter** on the rocks behind the dead cow.

After examining the initial **blood spatter**, you'll notice that there is a trail of **blood** leading away from the area. Activate your Witcher Sense and follow this trail a short distance until the tracks stop. At this point, examine the **dead dog** on the ground here.

At this point Geralt will figure out what he is up against.

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

Now that we know what the monster is, we'll now need to make our way back to the village of Fyresdal and speak with the elder, Vagn about it.

After the conversation, we'll be given a sheep. Geralt will need to herd it out of town and over to the objective marker close to the nest we spotted earlier. The sheep will run away from Geralt, so stay behind it and try to move in the opposite direction from where you want it to go in order to force it in the right direction.

As you approach the nest with the sheep the boss fight will begin in earnest.

> ## Skellige Isles Witcher Contracts (Continued)
>
> ##### Boss: Dragon (Forktail)
>
> The Dragon of Fyresdal, like most Forktails looks a lot more intimidating than it actually is and this fight is reminiscent to the Basilisk, Cockatrice and Griffin fights we've encountered earlier in the game. Fortunately, it acts in much the same way as the aforementioned monsters and as such the tactics remain essentially the same.
>
> Whilst it does have wings, for the most part it will attack from the ground and is quite fast. It will commonly use its speed and mouth full of teeth to lunge at Geralt from range or perform pouncing attacks. Similar to the Basilisks it is able to spit green projectiles at you which will poison Geralt if it makes contact. When it is in the air, it will try a swooping attack that can be easily rolled under/away from but will hit like a tonne of bricks if not avoided.
>
> The easiest way to take it down quickly is to use a combination of **AXII** to stun it, **IGNI** and **AARD** to dish out damage or **YRDEN** to slow its movements to get a few attacks in. It is also quite susceptible to damage from **Grapeshot Bombs**. Whilst in the air, hit it with crossbow bolts or **AARD** whilst it is flying to bring it crashing to the ground – run up and smash it a few times and then use your dodges and rolls to avoid its ground-based attacks whilst dishing out your own attacks when you see an opening. Repeat this process until it hits the dust.

Once the Dragon of Fyresdal is dead, loot the body for the **Forktail Trophy**, **Forktail Hide**, **Dragon Scales**, **Forktail Mutagen** and any other goodies it was carrying.

Now that the contract has been dealt with, we can return to the town of Fyresdal and speak with Vagn once again. Report on what has happened and during the conversation lie and exaggerate your claims to earn the full reward, if you are honest about the nature of the monster, the elder will only give you half of what was promised.

Either way the conversation goes down, once it ends, so to will the side-quest.

> **Note:** At this point, you'll earn **200XP** and **208 Crowns** for exaggerating or **102 Crowns** for being honest.

### Contract: Missing Son

**Start Location:** This quest can be acquired via a notice posted on the **Rannvaig** Notice board in western, central Ard Skellig.

After grabbing the side-quest from the notice board in Rannvaig, you can start it by heading into the town and speaking with an NPC named Odhen. You'll learn that his son is missing and it's up to us to find out what happened.

Following the conversation, we'll have a new objective marker on the map. Hop on your horse and ride to the next point of interest – a small search area encompassing a ruined castle keep.

As you arrive at the search area from the north, activate Witcher Sense and you can inspect the following:

- A set of **monster footprints** to examine
- A **corpse** by the footprints.

Once you have examined this pair of items, Geralt should be able to figure out what it is we'll be up against shortly. Rest, meditate and prepare yourself for a fight against a Fiend. When you are ready, enter the main section of the castle ruins to trigger the fight.

> ##### Boss: Morvudd (Fiend) – Part 1
>
> As you reach the next search area, you'll find yourself face to face with a Fiend. If you haven't fought one before, you're in for a tricky fight for a couple of reasons. Firstly, Fiends have a lot of health and also boast regenerative powers, so will regain lost health over time. Secondly they are able to cast a Hypnotize status ailment on Geralt, which will disable auto targeting for its duration.
>
> In addition to its abilities, the Fiend also has some nasty melee and leaping/charge attacks in addition to a nasty head-butt/gore manoeuvre. All of its attacks cover quite a large range and as such we'll need to dodge/roll away from them as much as possible.
>
> It can be damaged by bomb types including **Samum** and **Devil's Puffball** and will be temporarily stunned by **AXII**. Importantly, if you can interrupt the hypnotize cast (it will roar when it is starting to do this), things will be much easier. Other than that the fight boils down to standard operating procedure for the larger beasties we've fought to date – get a hit or two in, dodge, repeat.

After damaging it enough, the Fiend will run off.

At this stage, Geralt will find himself in the midst of a new search area. Throw on your Witcher Sense and you can inspect the following:

- The **body** at the base of the wall of the ruined castle.
- The **Fiend's footprints** leading away from the area.

Activate your Witcher Sense and follow the monsters trail down to the ledge below and then a short distance until the tracks stop at the edge of the river. At this point, cross to the far side and approach the next yellow search area in the ruins.

Again, switch on Witcher Senses and you can pick up the Fiend's trail. At this point, you'll also be close enough to the Fiend that if we turn on Witcher Senses, we'll be able to locate the red ripple in the environment representing the boss Follow this through until it leads you to another open area by some ruins in which we'll need to fight once again.

> ##### Boss: Morvudd (Fiend) – Part 2
>
> This time around, you'll be fighting the Fiend to the death. As mentioned above, the boss can be damaged effectively by bomb types including **Samum** and **Devil's Puffball** and will be temporarily stunned by **AXII**. Importantly, if you can interrupt the Fiend's hypnotize cast (it will roar when it is starting to do this), things will be much easier. Other than that the fight boils down to standard operating procedure for the larger beasties we've fought to date – get a hit or two in, dodge, repeat.

Once the Fiend boss is dead loot its body for the **Fiend Trophy** and **Fiend Mutagen** along with any other items of interest it is carrying around.

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

Before leaving the area, use Geralt's Witcher senses once again to locate a body by the wall at the northern end of the area. Inspect this to have your objectives update.

With the **Fiend Trophy** in your possession, return to Odhen in the village of Rannvaig to hand in the side-quest.

> **Note:** At this point, you'll earn **300XP** and **204 Crowns**.

> **Note:** For completing this Witcher Contract, you'll earn the achievement/trophy **‘Fiend or Foe?'**

## Kaer Morhen Secondary Quests

There are numerous hard to find side-quests located in and around the Witcher fortress of Kaer Morhen. These include a handful of short, out of the way story side-quests that add a bit of backstory to the Witcher order and their long-running shennaniganry.

Of particular note is one story-based side-quest involving a minor character that can be failed if you progress too far through the main game. As such, if you want to get all you can from the game, keep these in mind as you progress!

The quests involving main characters that can be failed include:

- **[Berengar's Blade](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Berengar's%20Blade)** – fails during **‘Isle of Mists'**.

The other, more minor quests here include:

- **[Greenhouse Effect](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Greenhouse%20Effect)**
- [**Monster Slayer**](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Monster%20Slayer)
- **[The Bastion](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#The%20Bastion)**
- **[The Witcher's Forge](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#The%20Witcher's%20Forge)**
- **[Trail of Echoes](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/xboxone/702759-the-witcher-3-wild-hunt/faqs/71896#Trail%20of%20Echoes)**

### Berengar's Blade

**Start Location:** In a chest within the walls of Kaer Morhen.

> **Note:** You must complete this side-quest before the **'Isle of Mists'** as it will fail during this quest.

> **Note:** If you have already found a **Diagram: Tor'Haerne** this side-quest cannot be triggered.

From the gate of Kaer Morhen, move through the area as if you were heading for the main keep - climb the first slope and follow the path around until you reach an open archway. From this archway there is another slope in front of you. At this point you'll want to look to the left to spot a pair of archways with iron bars. The left hand window is destroyed allowing you to enter. Inside you'll find a small sleeping area with a chest in the far, right corner from the entry point. Loot the chest for the Note - **Berengar's Notes** item.

With the notes in hand, keep moving along the set path until you reach the main keep of Kaer Morhen. Speak with Vesemir here and choose the appropriate conversation options that are now available to progress with the side-quest.

At this point we'll need to do a bit of exploring. Exit Kaer Morhen and head south until you reach the river. Follow the bank of the river to the east (or swim if you are keen!). There are several level 18 Drowning Dead and level 6 Bears on the banks of the river as you go so kill them if they get in your way. When the river bends and starts heading north, hop in the water and start swimming.

As you swim upriver, locate the small island on the eastern side of the river. Just beside this you'll find a cave entrance. Proceed into the cave until you see a larger room below. There's a powerful Chort inside and we'll need to take him out to continue. Take a moment to meditate and prepare for the fight before dropping down and attacking.

> ##### Boss: Chort
>
> The Chort is quite similar to the Fiend we fought earlier. The Chort itself has some fairly powerful melee and lunging attacks in addition to a nasty head-butt/gore manoeuvre. All of its attacks cover quite a large range and as such we'll need to dodge/roll away from them as much as possible. Make sure you use a lot of lateral movement during this fight to roll and circle the boss rather than retreating as otherwise you'll get cornered.
>
> The good news is that the Chort can be poisoned so using Devil's Puffball Bombs is a great idea – be sure to use them as much as possible to keep up the de-buff on the boss! Relict Oil will also provide you with a slight damage boost to make things a little easier for you.
>
> Other than keeping an eye on your environment and movement capabilities, the fight boils down to standard operating procedure for the larger beasties we've fought to date – get a hit or two in, dodge, repeat. Keep it up until the Chort falls.

Once the Chort has been defeated we'll need to use Witcher Sense to investigate the cave. Along the southern wall of the cavern you'll find a sack that glows red with Witcher Sense – this is our target! Loot it for **Diagram: Tor'Haerne**.

At this point, with the **Diagram** in hand the quest will be completed.

> **Note:** For completing this side-quest you'll earn **40XP**.

### Greenhouse Effect

**Start Location:** This can be very difficult to locate. To find it, look at your map. Note where the road south from Kaer Morhen fortress crosses the river for the first time. Just south of this is an island. Directly east of the island you should be able to make out a small trail on the map. Further to the east of this is another trail – at the end of this trail is where you'll start the quest.

> **Note:** You'll be unable to complete this quest unless you possess the **Eye of Nehaleni** given to you by Keira Metz during **‘Wandering in the Dark'**.

If you followed the instructions above (sorry if it was a little tricky to follow) you'll reach what looks like an inactive Point of Power – approach this to begin the quest. We'll need to search the area. Activate your Witcher Senses and you can inspect the following:

- Each of the **three obelisks** (inactive Places of Power).
- **Herb planters** in an alcove by the southernmost obelisk.
- **Sickle and pots** by a table in the alcove by the southernmost obelisk.

Whilst those are interesting, what we are really after is the illusion of a boulder in the alcove by the southernmost obelisk. Use your **Eye of Nehaleni** to remove the illusion and loot the **Document – Letter from the Resident Mage** that it was hiding.

At this point, we'll need to head into the Quest items tab of Geralt's inventory menu and read the **Letter from the Resident Mage.** This will update our objectives.

To restore the weather, we need to manipulate the obelisks. Hit one of them with a blast of **AARD** and the other two with a shot of **IGNI** (the specific stones don't seem to matter, just the combination of active signs).

Unfortunately, tinkering with the stones will summon a Chort – a smaller, less dangerous version of a Fiend. Use your Fiend tactics to take him down.

Once the Chort is dead, your objectives will tell you to return to this site in a few days. Alternatively, you can just head into the menu and Meditate for two days. When you have observed the change in the garden, the quest will be completed.

> **Note:** For completing the quest you'll earn **25XP**.

### Monster Slayer

**Start Location:** Using Witcher Sense, examine a corpse just to the west of the Lakeside Hut fast travel signpost (this signpost will be unlocked during **‘The Final Trial'**).

Once you find your first Drowner corpse, you should pick up the side-quest. At this point we can follow a trail of dead Drowners that leads north along the eastern shore of the lake from the Lakeshore hut.

Follow the shoreline into the inlet to the right. At the end, opposite the island just north of the hut you'll find a cave. The cave is populated by a Level 26 Rock Troll – head in and take care of him using your standard large enemy tactics.

After killing the Rock Troll enter the cave and activate your Witcher Senses. Here you can inspect the following points of interest:

- **Dead Drowner** by the right hand wall.
- **Document – ‘Old Letter'** inside the wreckage of the boat at the back of the cave.

At this point, we'll need to head into the Quest items tab of Geralt's inventory menu and read the **Old Letter.** This will complete the side-quest.

### The Bastion

**Start Location:** Approach the derelict keep built on the hill to the west of the Kaer moorhen fortress. Approaching it will unlock the quest.

> **Note:** You'll be unable to complete this quest unless you complete the **‘The Magic Lamp'** quest given by Keira Metz during **‘Wandering in the Dark'**.

Find the entrance/s to the keep and as you reach them you'll be attacked by a group of 4-5 Wraiths who have taken up residence in the courtyard of the ruin. Kill them all off and then make sure you have your **Magic Lamp** equipped. In this area we'll need to use the **Magic Lamp** to find Ghosts in and around the tower.

Ghosts appear almost like fireflies in the world so that you can spot their presence, they will only be properly revealed however once you shine the **Magic Lamp** on them. There are a couple of Ghosts we can find in the courtyard. They can be found in the following locations:

- Along the right hand wall after entering through the right hand gate.
- Along the left hand wall after entering through left hand gate (there is also a chest behind this containing some crafting designs (for me it contained **Design: Assassin's Gauntlets**, **Manuscript Page: Superior Maribor Forest**, money and some other goodies).
- Inside the left hand archway at the opposite end of the courtyard from the entry.

Once you have inspected those three Ghosts, look just inside the left hand entrance to the keep to find a ladder leading up to the walls. Climb to the battlements here. Follow them back to the left and climb up to the tower here to find another Ghost.

Loot the body here for **Boy's Remains**. Return to the courtyard below and you'll see that the Wraiths have reappeared. Kill them all again and then make your way over to the objective marker on the western side of the keep (where we found one of the Ghosts earlier).

Interact with the location to complete the quest.

> **Note:** For completing the quest you'll earn **25XP**.

### The Witcher's Forge

**Start Location:** In the main hall of the Kaer Morhen fortress, find the kitchen/feasting area and go through the door to the east to an octagon shaped room. Look for a pair of chests and loot the document **‘Monstrum, or a Portrayal of Witchers: Volume 2'** on top. This will start the quest.

> **Note:** You'll be unable to complete this quest unless you possess the **Eye of Nehaleni** given to you by Keira Metz during **‘Wandering in the Dark'**.

To actually start the quest, you'll need to head into your inventory, more specifically the Quest items tab and read the **Monstrum, or a Portrayal of Witchers: Volume 2.** This will update our objectives.

If you look at your map, you'll be given a new objective marker located to the southwest of the Kaer Morhen fortress. Make your way over here to discover the entrance to an Iron Mine (you'll also unlock a fast travel marker nearby).

Make your way through a cave until you reach a relatively large room. After a few moments here you'll be attacked by a Level 30 Earth Elemental . Treat him in the same way you would treat any of the other Elementals that you have fought previously **(YRDEN** is super effective at slowing him down!) – simply avoid his ranged and close quarters attacks whilst getting a hit or two in when you see the opportune moment.

After the fight, examine the small altar nearby with the carved stone book. This will open a door to the left. Enter and after looting your fill, approach the forge at the back of the room. Activate Witcher Sense and inspect the bellows here for a conversation.

> ##### Boss: Ifrit (Fire Elemental )
>
> The Fire Elemental can activate a fiery aura that will cause fire damage to Geralt if he stays too close for too long. In addition, the Elemental can dish out some nasty close range melee attacks and will regularly punch the ground causing an area of effect shockwave if you stay too close. He also has ranged capabilities with and will also shoot out a wave of fire in a line directly in front of him as well as tossing fireballs that can explode on impact causing a little splash damage if you are too close to them.
>
> The fight is tough for one reason, the awkward environment makes it difficult to approach the boss without him being able to attack first.
>
> Whilst he sounds tough, he is actually not all that tricky to take down. Using **AARD** will temporarily remove his fiery aura; disabling his fire-based AOE smash and allowing you to pretty much treat him as a regular large enemy. **YRDEN** is also helpful in that it will slow him down to make him more manageable. Once he is in the weakened state, we'll need to wait for an opening in its attacks. Fortunately most of its attacks are quite obviously telegraphed and there is a small window after any attack he does.

Following the fight, you can approach the wall behind the bellows and use your **Eye of Nehaleni** to remove the illusion here. Enter the secret compartment to find three chests we can loot for a unique sword **Tir Tochair Blade** and a piece of armour **Eternal Fire Executioner's Armour**.

Entering the hidden area here will complete the quest.

> **Note:** For completing the quest you'll earn **25XP**.

### Trail of Echoes

**Start Location:** To the northwest of Kaer Morhen fortress, you'll see a path leading into the hills. Follow this to find the entrance to a cave we'll need to explore for the quest.

> **Note:** This appears to be a non-official side-quest and as such does not have its own journal entry. it does however give you a lot of backstory on Witchers and earns you a bit of **XP**, so is definitely worth checking out.

Make your way over to the location described above and as you work your way along the dirt path towards the entrance marker, note that you should be careful as the entrance is guarded by a group of 6-8 Harpies.

Upon entering the cave, move through the set passage, you'll run across a Nekker Warrior and a Rotfiend. Once they are dealt with, you can proceed further into the cave and reach a laboratory type area populated by a group of four Nekker Warriors. Again clear them out.

In the lab here, we can search the area. Activate your Witcher Senses and you can inspect the following:

- One of **the tables for mutations** located throughout the lab.
- One of the **pots of mutagens** in the area.
- **Broken contraption** near the stairs at the back of the lab.
- Large **wooden vat** for seeping herbs at the back of the cave.
- **Barrel** opposite the vat at the back of the cave.
- **Document – ‘Trial of the Grasses Registry Tome'** on one of the mutation tables at the back of the cave.

The document is most important here. Loot this to earn some XP.

> **Note:** At this point you'll earn **25XP**.

There are also quite a few chests to be looted in here you for some good stuff (mine included **Diagram: Eirlithrad**). Looting the cave will essentially complete this non-official side-quest.

## Alchemy Guide: Potions, Decoctions and Oils

Alchemy plays a major role in The Witcher games and it should come as no surprise that it makes a return in The Witcher III: Wild Hunt. The game's alchemy allows Geralt to craft three different types of useable items that will assist him in numerous ways by supplementing his health, improving his combat abilities, applying passive bonus damages, removing de-buffs and even allowing him to see in the dark (along with many, many more of course).

In order to take advantage of alchemy, players must first find and unlock recipes either through completing quests, purchasing them from merchants or finding them stashed away in one of the millions of lootable objects scattered across the expansive game world. These recipes, once acquired can be crafted from the Alchemy tab of the menu.

Fortunately, after these formulae have been crafted for the first time, Geralt will remember how to make them (he's good like that) and as long as you have a sufficient amount of alcohol in your possession, he will automatically create a fresh batch every time you meditate.

The different types of alchemy formulae include:

- **Potions** – A wide range of different potions can be used to assist Geralt both in and out of combat. Potions offer up a wide ranging assortment of effects that can allow breathing in poisoned areas, regenerating health and increasing damage output (among others). Using potions will increase **Geralt's Toxicity bar** (filling this too much will result in a loss of health).
- **Decoctions** – Decoctions are special potions with more powerful effects that give bonus attributes, perks or qualities related to specific enemy types. Typically decoctions are found in the environment and in my experience you'll locate them near where you fight new enemy types for the first time. Using decoctions will greatly increase **Geralt's Toxicity bar** (filling this too much will result in a loss of health).
- **Oils** – Oils are used to enhance Geralt's weapons temporarily. For the most part these are used to increase attack damage or efficiency during combat and are often most effectively used when fighting against specific enemy types.
- **Bombs** – There are a range of bombs that Geralt can craft with numerous effects. They can be equipped and are utilised like grenades.

### Potions

Potions can be consumed to enhance Geralt with numerous beneficial effects. These range from temporarily increasing base attributes, replenishing health after taking damage, seeing in the dark and a number of other useful and interesting effects to assist you in a number of tasks.

The majority of potions have three tiers of quality – Standard, Enhanced and Superior. Each successive increase in quality tier will also provide a boost to the potion itself either improving its effectiveness, adding additional effects or increasing the quantity of potions able to be held in Geralt's inventory at once without meditating.

Most potions can either be purchased from Herbalists, Alchemy Supply shops or in chests hidden throughout the game world.

> **Note:** I have listed the locations of some of the potions in the 'Notes' column of the table below. If you find any additional locations where the potions can be found/purchased, please head down to the contact section and let me know!

### Decoctions

The Witcher III: Wild Hunt's Decoctions are very powerful versions of potions with much more potent effects. Their recipes often call for specific **Mutagens**, items that will only drop from specific powerful monsters (usually from bosses or Witcher Contracts) once they have been killed. Although these effects are stronger, they also consume a much higher amount of Toxicity (normal potions are like 20%, these are 70-80% no matter the potion). They also last at least 30 minutes.

Most of the Decoction formulae are harder to find than the potions, but a vast majority of them are still sold by alchemists throughout the world or often hidden away in chests or crates, often close to where you encounter the enemy for the first time (e.g early in the game when you come across your first Foglet, there is usually a chest nearby with the formula in it). There are also a select few Decoctions that can be purchased from vendors.

> **Note:** The contents of chests are random so it is unlikely that you'll find the same Decoctions in the same chests as other people or across play throughs. As such chest locations would be pretty pointless to include in the below table.

> **Note:** I have listed the locations of some of the Decoctions in the 'Notes' column of the table below. If you find any additional locations where the Decoctions can be found/purchased, please head down to the contact section and let me know!

### Oils

Oils are special buffs that can be applied to Geralt's swords to imbue a passive bonus. These are most often associated with increasing damage towards specific creature types. Most of the Oil formulae are sold by alchemists throughout the world or often hidden away in chests or crates throughout the world.

As with the other formulae and crafting items, once you have discovered the recipe, simply acquire the necessary ingredients (listed in the Alchemy menu) and then create it from the Alchemy menu. Fortunately Oils do not expires so once you have crafted them once, they'll stay in your inventory for ever, and ever, and ever.

There are three levels of Oil and the level of Oil will determine the bonus given. They are as such:

- Standard: Additional 10% bonus damage to affected creatures.
- Enhanced: Additional 25% bonus damage to affected creatures.
- Superior: Additional 50% bonus damage to affected creatures.

The Standard and Enhanced Oil recipes are commonly found in chests scattered throughout the game world and from specific Herbalist/Alchemy Supply vendors. The Superior Oil recipes can only be found in chests.

> **Note:** The contents of chests are random so it is unlikely that you'll find the same Oils in the same chests as other people or across play throughs. As such chest locations would be pretty pointless to include in the below table.

> **Note:** I have not listed the locations of some of the Oils in the table below. If you find any locations where the Oils can be found/purchased, please head down to the contact section and let me know!

### Bombs

In addition to consumables, Geralt is also able to craft various types of bombs which can be used to inflict high damage and lingering effects on a range of different enemy types. Bomb effects range from generating poison clouds, freezing enemies in their tracks, adding status ailment debuffs such as poison or burning and in general – just causing a great deal of damage to specific enemy types.

As with potions and oils, bombs have three tiers of quality – Standard, Enhanced and Superior. Each successive increase in quality tier will also provide some kind of boost to the bomb itself either improving its effectiveness, adding additional effects or increasing the quantity that Geralt is able to carry at once without meditating.

As with the Oils, the Standard and Enhanced Bomb recipes are commonly found in chests scattered throughout the game world and from specific Herbalist/Alchemy Supply vendors. The Superior Bomb recipes can only be found in chests.

> **Note:** The contents of chests are random so it is unlikely that you'll find the same Bombs in the same chests as other people or across play throughs. As such chest locations would be pretty pointless to include in the below table.

> **Note:** I have not listed the locations of some of the Bombs in the table below. If you find any locations where the Bombs can be found/purchased, please head down to the contact section and let me know!

## Gwent Guide

In addition to tonnes of narrative driven side-content and the lengthy main quest line, The Witcher III: Wild Hunt also includes a surprisingly deep, fun and addictive card game going by the name of Gwent. During your adventure, Geralt can find cards, build a deck, try his hand against Merchants and Innkeepers as well as hunting down and playing various top-tier players to cement his place as the best Gwent player in the history of the universe (or something).

You can first play Gwent upon arriving in the town of White Orchard. In the tavern, you'll want to speak with the Scholar at the table by the bar. He'll introduce the game to you and initiate a short tutorial on how to play before plunging you headlong into your first match.

This short section the guide is dedicated to outlining the basics of the game, giving you a few hints and tips as to how to win and include a list of every card in the game and where to find them all.

### How to Play

Gwent is played across three rounds with the winner being the first player to win two rounds. At the start of the match, players draw 10 cards from their deck. At this point, if you are unhappy with your cards there is the option to discard two cards from the hand to draw another two. Once the drawing is done, players must utilise the hand given across the three rounds of the game. As such, card and resource management is very important!

Essentially the Gwent Board is divided into three sections for each player in which they can play Infantry (Front), Ranged (Middle) and Siege (Back) unit cards. These unit cards each have an attack value attributed to them and the player with the highest total attack score at the end of each round is victorious.

Whilst it sounds simple enough, there's plenty of depth to the game. It's not a simple matter of placing attacking cards onto the board as there are numerous cards that have special abilities with a range of effects that can drastically impact what happens on the table. Additionally, there are four different decks available (Northern Realms, Monsters, Nilfgaardian Empire and Scoia'tael) and depending on the faction selected, there are certain special abilities granted to players which can further influence the flow of the game.

Players are able to pass/withdraw and sit out of a round at any time once they have placed their first card. When one player has withdrawn, the other can still play as many cards as they like until they too decide to withdraw. The scores are then tallied and the winner of the round determined.

### Card Types and Special Abilities

#### Card Types

Gwent features a range of different card types, each with their own unique uses and abilities. The following list outlines the various types of cards in the game and what makes each unique.

- **Unit Cards:** These are the basic attacking cards and will be the most abundant of the cards you come across. They come in three flavours – Infantry, Ranged and Siege.
- **Leader Cards:** Leader Cards are the boss card of your deck. They cannot be placed onto the table, but sit in the background. Each leader card has four variations, each which has a unique special ability that can be utilised once a game to help turn the tide in your favour.
- **Hero Cards:** Gwent's Hero Cards are the superheroes of the game and can be described as more powerful variants of your standard unit cards. They feature useful abilities such as being completely unaffected by weather effects and usually have a high attack scores. The Hero Cards are locked into specific factions, so if you have a deck set up for said faction, as you find these cards it's a good idea to incorporate them into your Gwent deck and replace your standard units as you go.
- **Neutral Cards:** Neutral cards are Hero cards that do not belong to a faction and therefore can be incorporated into a deck featuring any faction. As with heroes they are unaffected by weather cards and have a high attack value. In addition to being wildcards and being available for any deck, they also usually feature useful buffing abilities to give your hand an edge.
- **Weather Cards:**When played right, weather cards can be extremely effective at nullifying damage from opponents, the problem is weather cards, when played will affect both sides of the board. There are four types of weather cards, with three associated with messing with different lines of the Gwent board and reducing the attack power of units affected to 1. These include:
  - **Biting Frost –** This will reduce the attack values of all infantry cards to 1.
  - **Impenetrable Fog -** This will reduce the attack values of all Ranged cards to 1.
  - **Torrential Rain –** This will reduce the attack values of all Siege Engine cards to 1.
  - **Clear Weather -** Clears all weather effects from the board.
- **Special Cards:** Similar to the weather cards, these special cards are one-off use cards that can cause a number effects to the current cards in play. Using them in the right conditions at the right time and they can change the entire tone of a round.These special cards include **:**
  - **Commander's Horn –** Doubles the strength of units on a given row.
  - **Decoy –** Switch this with a card on the table to return it to your hand.
  - **Scorch –** Destroys the highest value card/s in play. Will effect both sides of the table.

#### Card Special Abilities

In addition to their Strength values, many unit cards also have their own unique abilities which can be used to your advantage. Below you'll find a list of the special abilities that unit cards can have and a short description on what they'll do and how they can be used. The special abilities include:

- **Agile:** The Agile ability is a handy one to have on your unit cards as those with this ability can be placed in either the front or middle row of the table, potentially avoiding weather effect cards already in play. The Agile ability is especially is abundant in the Scoia'tael deck and
- **Medic:** Playing a card with the Medic ability will allow you to look at your discarded cards and select a previously used unit card to play onto the board instantly. Using a Medic card to revive another with the Medic ability will have that trigger as well, effectively enabling players to revive multiple unit cards in a single turn.
- **Morale Boost:** A unit card with this ability will increase the Strength of every other unit card in the same row by 1. It will not increase its own strength.
- **Muster:** Playing a card with the Muster ability will search your deck with any other cards with the same name and place them onto the board, in play instantly.
- **Spy:** The Spy ability is one of the better ones that a unit card can possess. When played, the Spy card is placed on your opponent's side of the board (and will count towards their total strength pool) and will allow you to draw two additional cards.
- **Tight Bond:** When a unit card with Tight Bond is placed next to a unit card with the same name, the Tight Bond ability works by doubling the strength of both unit cards.

### Constructing a Deck

There are four different deck factions in Gwent and each has their own unique cards, special abilities and heroes. There are 150 cards in total in the game that can be acquired through numerous means including beating other players and purchasing them from merchants who have cards in stock. Upon acquiring new cards it's a good idea to have look at your stock and see what you can do about deck building – the starter deck will only get you so far!

There are four factions, each with their own unique special ability. They include:

- **Nilfgaardian Empire –** Will win a round if it ends in a draw.
- **Northern Realms –** Players are able to draw an additional card upon winning a round.
- **Monsters**– A random unit card will remain on the board following each round.
- **Scoia'tael** – Players are able to choose who goes first.

When building a deck, it's a good idea to remember that a deck must contain at least 22 unit cards and this can feature up to 10 special cards. Making your deck as small as possible will increase your chances of drawing the better cards Geralt has at his disposal.

Below you'll find a breakdown of the four card factions in the game and a brief overview of what you can expect from playing with them:

#### Northern Realms

The Northern Realms will be your starting deck and once you get the hang of it and flesh it out with a few more high-profile cards, it is also arguably the best deck in the game. Some key elements to take into account when running with a Northern Realms deck:

Foltest is the Leader card of the Northern Realms deck and he has a number of useful abilities depending on the variant that you play with. They include:

- **The Steel-Forged -** Trigger a Scorch effect on back (Siege) row units if unit strength is 10 or greater.
- **The Siege Master -** Trigger a Commander's Horn effect on your back (Siege) row units.
- **Lord Commander of the North –** Trigger a Clear Weather effect to remove all weather cards in play.
- **King of Temeria -** Allows you to pick an Impenetrable Fog card from your deck and play it instantly.

Some general thoughts, tips and interesting stuff about playing with a Northern Realms deck:

- Winning a round using a Northern Realms deck will allow you to draw another card, which is good for keeping a card advantage over your opponent.
- The deck has three Spy ability cards which will allow you to draw a large number of cards if you get them all together. Couple these with Decoys and you have a recipe for success.
- Using a selection of cards with the Tight Bond ability (Blue Stripes Commando, Catapult, Crinfrid Reavers Dragon Hunter) and placing all of them in play at once will greatly boost your overall attack score. Couple a few Catapults with Tight Bond with with a Commander's Horn and Foltest's Siege Master ability allows the faction to quickly rack up some big strength numbers, making it a powerful opponent if used correctly.
- The Northern Realm deck has access to a few Medic cards. Using these to resurrect other Medic ability cards or discarded spy cards will again give you a distinct advantage.

#### Monsters

The Monsters deck is a tricky one to get a handle on because of its abundance of melee enemies and its focus on the muster special ability. It's also not a very good to begin with and you'll need to work towards getting a few heroes and monster cards in your deck before trying it out. Some key elements to take into account when running with a Monsters faction deck:

Eredin is the Leader card of the Monsters deck and he has a number of useful abilities depending on the variant that you play with. They include:

- **Destroyer of Worlds -** Pick a card from your discard pile and put it back into your hand
- **Bringer of Death -** Discard two cards from your hand and in turn choose and draw 1 card from the deck.
- **King of the Wild Hunt –** Trigger a Commander's Horn effect on your front (infantry) row units.
- **Commander of the Red Riders -** Pick any weather card from the deck and play it instantly.

Some general thoughts, tips and interesting stuff about playing with a Monsters deck:

- Winning a round using a Monsters deck will allow one random unit card to stay in play for the next round. As such you'll want to try and win the first round to make use of that advantage.
- Monsters have no medic or spies and no real ability to draw cards so will rely on Muster to pull cards into play. This is very difficult to maintain over three rounds.
- Muster is the core of the Monsters faction. To make good use of the Muster ability, you'll want to have duplicates of the cards featuring that ability ( **Vampires, Arachas, Crones, Ghouls, Nekkers**). If you have the entire suite of like-named Muster cards, a single play can pull 3-5 Cards into play and place huge strength numbers (up to 21 with all five **Vampires**) onto the board in a single turn.
- As the majority of the Monster deck is melee, frontline units it's a good idea to hold onto a Clear Weather card in your deck to remove the inevitable Biting Frost cards opponents will no doubt roll out.

#### Nilfgaardian Empire

The Nilfgaardian Empire plays in a similar fashion to the Northern Realms deck and can utilise similar strategies revolving around the use of Decoys and Spy cards. Some key elements to take into account when running with a Nilfgaardian Empire deck:

Emyhr is the Leader card of the Nilfgaardian Empire deck and he has a number of useful abilities depending on the variant that you play with. They include:

- **The Relentless** \- Allows you to draw a card from the opponent's discard pile.
- **The White Flame** \- Allows you to cancel out your opponent's Leader ability when used.
- **The Emperor of Nilfgaard** \- Enables you to observe three random cardsin your opponent's hand.
- **His Imperial Majesty** \- Allows you to pick an Torrential Rain card from your deck and play it instantly.

Some general thoughts, tips and interesting stuff about playing with a Nilfgaardian Empire deck:

- The special ability of the Nilfgaardian Empire deck is that you technically do not have to win any round as you'll receive the win in the event of a draw.
- As with the Northern Realms faction, this deck has three Spy ability cards which will allow you to draw a large number of cards if you get them all together. Couple these with Decoys and you have a recipe for success.
- The Nilfgaardian Empire decks has access to a lot of Medic cards. Using these to resurrect other Medic ability cards or discarded spy cards will again give you a distinct advantage.

#### Scoia'tael

The Scoia'tael faction, like the Monsters deck is a little tricky to use. Whilst Northern Realms and Nilfgaardian Empire can get away with similar tactics, Scoia'tael provide something different to try out and the deck features a good mix of Muster, Agile and Medic cards. It should be noted that the deck itself is definitely not a starter deck and will need quite a few cards (and hopefully heroes as well) to choose from before you can construct a decent deck that will have you winning with regularity. Some key elements to take into account when running with a Scoia'tael deck:

Francesca is the Leader card of the Scoia'tael deck and she has a number of useful abilities depending on the variant that you play with. They include:

- **Queen of Dol Blathanna** – Trigger a Scorch effect on front (Infantry) row units if unit strength is 10 or greater.
- **The Beautiful** \- Trigger a Commander's Horn effect on your middle (Ranged) row units.
- **Daisy of the Valley** – You can draw one additional card at the start of the game.
- **Pureblood Elf** – Allows you to pick a Biting Frost card from your deck and play it instantly.

Some general thoughts, tips and interesting stuff about playing with a Scoia'tael deck:

- The Scoia'tael faction's special ability will allow you to choose which player will play first each round. This is a good opportunity to force your opponent to start so that you can get an early insight into what kind of strategy they are likely to use. Alternatively, you can start laying down big cards first in an attempt to set the tone of the round.
- As with the Monsters deck, the Scoia'tael deck has no Spy cards and no other ability to draw cards so you'll need to rely on Medic reviving and Muster to pull cards into play.
- The central focus of the Scoia'tael deck is the Agile ability. This will allow you to place most of the faction's unit cards on either the front or middle rows of the table. This is useful when stacking unit cards for a damage boost via a Commander's Horn and is also useful in mitigating or minimising the impact of weather cards on your units.
- To make good use of the Muster ability, you'll want to have duplicates of the cards featuring that ability ( **Elven Skirmishers, Dwarven Skirmishers, Havekar Smugglers**). If you have the entire suite of like-named Muster cards, a single play can pull three cards and a huge boost to your strength numbers (up to 15 with three **Havekar Smugglers**) onto the board in a single turn.

### General Hints and Tips

Here are some general tips to help introduce you to the world of Gwent. I'll be the first to admit that I myself am not all that great at the side-game but I found the following extremely useful and would have liked to have known/figured them out much sooner.

> **Note:** If you have a handy tip or hint, scroll down to the contact section and swing me an email.

- Gwent is not the easiest game to get the hang of, especially if you aren't a fan of card games. Spend some time on low/no wager matches so that you can learn the ins and outs of each deck and special abilities.
- You'll want to experiment with deck building and once you have enough cards, try playing with the different factions until you find one that you feel comfortable using.
- Be sure to visit every merchant/innkeeper when you visit a new area and purchase all of the Gwent cards in their inventory. Playing against merchants/innkeepers and winning for the first time will also net you a random card to add to your collection.
- Don't be afraid to pass and gift the opponent the first round. You only have limited cards, so make sure you don't waste them if you have no chance of winning!
- There's no shame in going into a card duel for the sole purpose of losing, just so that you can see what kind of deck, cards and strategy an opponent might be running. Afterwards, prepare your deck accordingly for a rematch.
- Try to get your opponent to waste cards! They can't win the latter rounds if they have no cards left! A good technique is to move some high value cards and decoys into your deck. Placing a high attack value card on the field to drive up their card numbers and then use decoy to return it to your hand before passing.
- Substitute standard unit cards with Hero/Neutral cards as soon as you get them. Hero/Neutral cards are more powerful and also less likely to be susceptible to special abilities.
- Unit cards with the Spy ability are an excellent addition to any deck. Although you'll place them on the opponent's side of the table, they allow you to draw two cards which is a nice bonus. If you follow that up with a well-timed weather card, this will essentially mitigate your opponent's advantage.
- If an opponent places a unit card with the Spy ability on your side of the table, use a Decoy card to grab it for yourself!
- Muster is a highly useful ability, but for it to be most effective, you'll need to load your deck with cards of the same variety whose name matches the unit cards with the Muster ability.
- Medics can be quite powerful - using a Medic card to revive another card with the Medic ability will have that trigger as well, effectively enabling players to revive multiple unit cards in a single turn.

### Obtaining New Gwent Cards

There are a total of 198 Gwent Cards in the game with 120 unique cards. In addition to this there are an additional 75 duplicate cards. Geralt will start the game with a Northern Realms deck featuring 20 unique cards but this deck can be easily upgraded or switched out with another faction once you have collected enough cards to put together a viable deck. If you want to complete the side-quest **‘Gwent: Collect ‘em All!'** and the associated achievement/trophy **‘Card Collector'**

First of all there are 10 missable Gwent cards, so make sure you keep an eye out for these as you progress through the game. If you miss obtaining these during your play through, you'll be unable to earn this achievement/trophy and will need to either load an earlier save file or start afresh and try again.

- Play and with the Gwent Tournament at the masquerade ball during Triss's **‘A Matter of Life and Death'** side-quest. There are three missable Gwent cards to be won.
- Complete Zoltan's side-quest **‘A Dangerous Game'** before sending him to Kaer Morhen. There are three missable Gwent cards to be found here.
- Win the Gwent Tournament at the Passiflora during the ‘ **High Stakes'** side-quest. If you lose, you'll be locked out of challenging the remaining players so be sure to save before you attempt this one! There are four missable Gwent cards to be won.
- Several characters disappear during the game so you should complete the **'Gwent: Old Pals'**, **'Gwent: Big City Players'** and **'Gwent: Skellige Style'** side-quests ASAP to ensure you grab all of the rare cards on offer.
- Although it appears that beating the Bloody Baron in Gwent is missable if you complete the **‘Return to Crookback Bog'** side-quest. If you return to his office in the keep at Crow's Perch, you'll find his unique Gwent Card on the desk.

In general, you can earn cards from the following actions:

- Geralt will start the game with 33 cards broken down as such: 22 x Northern Realm cards, 7 x Neutral Cards and 4 x Leader cards (one for each faction).
- Completing the Gwent side-quests will earn you 22 unique (and powerful) cards to add to your deck. The side-quests include: **‘Gwent: Velen Players'**, **‘Gwent: Playing Innkeepers'**, **‘Gwent: Big City Players'**, **‘Gwent: Old Pals'** and **‘Gwent: Skellige Style'**.
- Gwent Cards can be purchased from innkeepers or Traders. It's a good idea to purchase everything available when you come across a Gwent salesperson, even if you already own the cards as this will give you more options when it comes to deck building.
- Playing innkeepers, traders and other challenges will usually net you a new card from a large pool of random card rewards the first time you beat them.
